idea: Fix the seven accumulated P1/P2 super-* followups in one run (literal human request: "lets $ht handoff to claude-2 all p1 and p2 fixes, at once, using /super-auto in fully autonomous mode, both roasts on"): super-plan-2s3 (P1, complete review-report producer/extractor contract: narrative finding sections and roast PR Unverified-nits prose fail extraction even when canonical footers parse; preserve findings and original generations, validate output, bounded format-only repair, meaningful contract cases), super-plan-rsc (scope review provenance to the relevant captured range; an unchanged incomplete old run must not block a product.txt-only later range; distinguish roast be183064..tip from final-review upstream5608de06..tip ranges), super-plan-tco (canonical super-auto/super-design callers carry evidence-backed reviewReadiness to super-roast; missing evidence stays unknown), super-plan-row (caller persists existing finding source/link/route/disposition records including identity/status of pending human escalations; never group on display key alone, infer repaired from landed beads, or auto-approve human acceptance changes), super-plan-v94 (reused existing stacked branch/worktree must still run current prerequisite reconciliation, preserving own committed/dirty work, no blind reset/deletion), super-plan-1s3 (context-preflight 200K vs run-profile 1M on the identical explicit opus 5.5 model id; consistent resolution preserving overrides and unknowns, no speculative routing), super-plan-zhk (investigate why project AGENTS.md commit policy forbidding AI co-author lines escaped into six commits; implement the smallest justified generic project-commit-policy propagation/review correction or report root uncovered; no global hardcoded trailer ban, no history rewrite). Authoritative detail: bd show <id> for each. Scope: smallest change covering all seven, in existing scripts/prompts/contracts/tests, no new workflow abstraction; no P3 super-plan-618. Skill edits follow writing-skills with behavior evals; tests prove behavior. Main coordinator owns merge into main and release.

- finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"roast-code/round-1","artifact":"2026-10-28-review-continuity-followups-roast-pr-1.md","kind":"roast","verdict":"Should-fix (12 confirmed) [low coverage] [review readiness incomplete]","occurrences":[{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":1,"key":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:140","text":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:140 — The new fence tracking in `report-verdict --complete` has no unclosed-fence error. A single stray or unbalanced fence line inside an item makes every later recognized heading count as continuation text, and the command exits 0 with Untested scope and Deferred OK items silently filed as Must-fix occurrences and the sections themselves gone. Base handled the same file correctly. [lanes: correctness, simplicity-design]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: Reproduced by the reproduce and ground seats on head 5e427d04: a Must-fix item with an unclosed triple-backtick continuation line, followed by `## Untested scope`, `## Deferred OK` and `## Verdict`; `--complete --source format-check` exits 0 with 4 occurrences, all in \"Must fix before landing\"; item 2's text ends in `\\n\\n## Untested scope` and the last item absorbs `## Verdict\\n\\nnot ready`. Base 444f86b2 has no fence tracking (`git show 444f86b2:skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict | grep -i fence` is empty) and parses the same file correctly. report-verdict:140-141 toggles `inFence` on any line matching `^ {0,3}(```|~~~)`, ignores fence type and length, and nothing checks it at EOF; assembly-archive.cjs:26-28 has the same toggle. The refute seat dissented: design §4.1(d) defines the fence as running \"to the next such line\" and says nothing about an unclosed fence, so the toggle matches the spec text. The two confirming seats answered that §4.1(c) reserves exit 4 for content that fails the grammar and the change's stated goal is to replace silent misfiling with a loud failure; a silent exit 0 that drops two sections is that defect class.\n  fix-shape hint: at EOF, if `inFence` is still set, raise the grammar error (exit 4, \"unclosed fence\"); mirror the check in assembly-archive.cjs strictDraft; optionally require the closing fence to match the opening marker."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":2,"key":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:229","text":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:229 — `report-verdict --repair-check` accepts a repair that puts a `- ` marker on an indented continuation line of an existing finding. That splits one finding into two `--complete` occurrences (a new spurious Confirmed-findings occurrence, with shifted positions), which contradicts the design's claim that a repair cannot move a continuation line or change finding content. [lanes: correctness]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: Reproduced by the reproduce and ground seats at head: a Confirmed-findings item followed by the indented line `  Evidence: the call drops the error.`; a repair that rewrites it as `-   Evidence: the call drops the error.` passes `--repair-check` (exit 0); `--complete` on the repair then yields an extra occurrence keyed `Evidence: the call drops the error.` and the real finding's text loses its evidence. Cause: line 229 clause `(!item.test(a) && b === '- ' + a)`; `item` is `/^ ?(?:[-*]|[0-9]+[.)])[ \\t]/`, so a two-space-indented continuation counts as \"not an item\". Check (iii) compares only `--keys` output, which for roast reports reads `- [SEV]` lines only, so the new untagged item changes no key. Design §4.1(c) says the repair \"cannot ... move a continuation line\" and that finding content is never edited. The refute seat confirmed at Nit (needs an over-editing repairer). The spurious occurrences feed `finding-source` records that scope-dispositions counts as actionable.\n  fix-shape hint: in check (iv), allow the `- ` prefix only when the original line has no leading whitespace; this and the heading case below share one guard."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":3,"key":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:223; skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:229","text":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:223; skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:229 — `--repair-check` rule (iv) accepts a repair that demotes a heading or bold label into a list item, which reclassifies findings and adds new occurrences in non-Confirmed roast sections that check (iii) never compares; no test covers the case. [lanes: api-contract, testing]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: All three seats reproduced at head 5e427d04. `sed 's/^## Rejected (with reason)$/- &/' tests/super-code/fixtures/report-verdict/roast-template-shaped.md > dem.md; report-verdict --repair-check <fixture> dem.md` exits 0; `--complete` on dem.md then places the literal heading and the refuted `[Should-fix] src/e.sh:8` under \"Beyond remainder cap (count only)\". The ground seat got the same with `## Unverified nits (spot-checked)` demoted: nit d.sh:2 moves into \"Rejected (with reason)\" and a bogus occurrence appears. Cause: the `(!item.test(a) && b === '- ' + a)` clause at line 229 accepts any non-item line, headings included, bypassing the `roastWord` guard in `demoted()` (line 222). Check (iii) cannot see it because roast `--keys` covers only Confirmed findings. Design §4.1(c): the repair \"cannot ... add or rename a section heading\" and \"every original line keeps its section because no section boundary changes\". tests/super-code/test-report-verdict.sh:554-597 has \"heading renamed\" and \"heading added\" cases but none for demotion; deleting `!roastWord.test(m[1])` fails no test.\n  fix-shape hint: restrict the `'- ' + a` branch to lines that are not ATX headings, not bold labels and not indented continuations; add rejection tests for a demoted `##` heading and a demoted recognized-word bold label."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":4,"key":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/dispatch-text.mjs:422","text":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/dispatch-text.mjs:422 — The unbounded outOfRange list is written in full into generated-input-provenance.md, which the assembler copies verbatim into every digest.md; on this repo that is roughly 800-900KB of informational text per assembly, growing with history. [lanes: premortem, hygiene-docs]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: dispatch-text.mjs:422 appends `...outOfRange.map(g => '- Outside captured range (informational; ...)' + g)` with no cap or summary. coordinator.js:2565 defines the digest's provenance section as \"the complete contents of generated-input-provenance.md, verbatim\"; both the final-review and roast-code profiles include it; assemblePrompt's threshold is 80000 (coordinator.js:2562) and coordinator.js:2395-2397 turns `fits:no` into split or capped mode. Two scout runs at 5e427d04 on 444f86b2..5e427d04 produced 1430 outOfRange entries, a provenance file of 894,004 and 894,415 bytes, and a review-inputs.json of about 1.3MB; 1356 entries come from one fixture run.md under 2026-10-16-workflow-reliability. The branch commits an archived generated-input-provenance.md of 1,156,455 bytes under docs/superpowers/runs/2026-10-28-review-continuity-followups/reviews/inv-20261028T162628/. The ground seat confirmed the code path, the verbatim rule, the threshold and the committed artifact by reading. The refute seat dissented: design §4.2 puts outOfRange in the provenance digest, names no cap, and the same texts were previously gap lines in the same file. The confirming seats answered that on base those were blocking gaps (exit 3, no assembly happened), so this change is what delivers roughly 11x the assembler threshold of informational text to every reviewer, undercutting the stated goal that an old run elsewhere must not impede a product-only review.\n  fix-shape hint: write a count plus a bounded sample (first N entries) into the provenance digest and keep the full list only in review-inputs.json."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":5,"key":"[Should-fix] tests/super-auto/review-continuity-e2e.sh:113","text":"[Should-fix] tests/super-auto/review-continuity-e2e.sh:113 — The S2 and S5 'report-status prints no finding evidence or review collection qualifier' assertions in review-continuity-e2e.sh pass even when the finding projection they exist to verify never ran: the exit code of report-status is not checked, and the qualifier report-status prints when the projection fails ('finding projection unavailable — retain known findings') matches neither negative glob. [lanes: test-causality-lifecycle]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: Lines 113 and 202 run `run bash \"$RS\" ...` and then only `case \"$out$err\" in *'finding evidence'*|*'review collection'*) fail ;; *) pass`; `$code` is never read and no positive projection output (`outstanding findings: 7`) is asserted; a grep of the file finds no assertion on `outstanding findings` or `finding projection unavailable`. skills/super-auto/scripts/report-status (~line 613) prints `finding projection unavailable — retain known findings` when `scope-dispositions --project` fails; that string matches neither glob, and a crash that prints nothing also passes. The scout reproduced it by replacing scope-dispositions with `exit 1`: S4 failed 4 times while `[PASS] S2 ...` and `[PASS] S5 ...` still printed. The seats confirmed from source; none re-ran the stub. Severity: reproduce seat Nit (S4 still catches a broken projection), refute and ground seats Should-fix; Should-fix kept because the assertion exists to tie the missing qualifier to a projection that ran, and it does not.\n  fix-shape hint: assert `$code` is 0 and assert the positive projection output (or the absence of `finding projection unavailable`) alongside the existing negative globs, at both lines."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":6,"key":"[Nit] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2654; skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2652","text":"[Nit] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2654; skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2652 — The generated drift-check script has no `set -e` and never checks `git checkout -q --detach $integ`; if the detach fails, the stack merges and `rebase --onto` land on the task branch itself and the script still prints a success `REBUILT:` line with a wrong base. [lanes: correctness, premortem, concurrency-async, observability]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: coordinator.js:2652-2654 at head: the driftCheck array is joined with newlines and run with plain `bash`, no `set -e`; `git checkout -q --detach $integ` has no `|| fail`; `newBase=$(git rev-parse HEAD)` and `echo \"REBUILT: base=$newBase oldHead=$oldHead\"` are unconditional; only the merges and the rebase carry `|| fail`. If the detach fails, HEAD stays on `$br`, the `stack:` merges commit onto the task branch, newBase becomes the branch tip, and the agent reads a success line. Design §4.5 rejects that layout (base above the task's own commits). `fail()` also runs `git checkout -q $br` unchecked and always prints \"restored to old head\". The drift check is absent on base. All three seats confirmed by reading; none ran the failure; the trigger (checkout failing after the clean-status check passed, e.g. a stale index.lock or a .tmp collision) is rare.\n  fix-shape hint: append `|| fail checkout` to the detach line, or put `set -e` at the top of the script, and have `fail()` verify the checkout before claiming restoration."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":7,"key":"[Nit] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2646","text":"[Nit] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2646 — The new reuse drift-check reads stack-parent branch refs that the concurrently running merge lane can delete, so a parent that lands while this setup agent is running turns into a spurious SETUP_FAILED that misreports a merge conflict and tells the agent not to retry. [lanes: concurrency-async]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: coordinator.js:1652 snapshots `att.parents` from implDone at attempt start; the prompt is built lazily at dispatch. The drift script runs `git merge-base --is-ancestor task-P $br || drift=1` and then `git merge --no-ff -m \"stack: P\" task-P || fail P` with no SHA snapshot; on a missing ref `fail()` prints `SETUP_FAILED: P conflicted (); ... nothing reset` with an empty file list, driftText forbids retry, and line 1801 maps SETUP_FAILED to BLOCKED. Line 2882 passes `--keep-branch` only when `reviewBead` is truthy, so the parent branch is deleted at merge only on the review-bead null-dispatch path. All three seats agree the race needs a BOTH-exist restart plus a parent landing without a review bead inside the setup window; work is preserved and integ already holds the parent's code. Severity: reproduce and refute seats Nit, ground seat Should-fix; Nit kept because the outcome is a recoverable BLOCKED task in an already degraded run of a single-operator tool.\n  fix-shape hint: in the drift script, treat a parent whose ref no longer resolves as already landed (skip the ancestor check and the merge), or resolve parent SHAs once at the top of the script."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":8,"key":"[Nit] skills/super-auto/scripts/scope-dispositions:185","text":"[Nit] skills/super-auto/scripts/scope-dispositions:185 — The new unrouted-decision check matches a scope-filter key against the `--complete` occurrence key by exact string equality, but the two keys are normalized differently. A roast entry with whitespace before ` — ` gets a false `scope-filter decision without finding-route` gap (exit 3) even when its route is recorded. [lanes: correctness, testing]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: report-verdict `keys_roast` (lines 368-377) cuts at the first \" — \" and runs `sub(/[ \\t]+$/, \"\")`; the `--complete` occurrence key at report-verdict:130 is `first.split(' — ')[0]`, untrimmed; scope-dispositions:185 compares `o.value.key === key` exactly. For `- [Nit] b.js:2  — claim` the occurrence key is `[Nit] b.js:2 ` and the decision key is `[Nit] b.js:2`, so a routed decision counts as unrouted and `--project` exits 3. The decision regex at :179 also requires a trailing space after `in-scope|punch-list —`, so a line without it is skipped silently. All three seats confirmed by reading; none reproduced. The trigger is malformed spacing and the failure is loud, not silent.\n  fix-shape hint: trim the occurrence key in `--complete` (or both sides at the comparison) and make the trailing space in the decision regex optional."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":9,"key":"[Nit] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2655","text":"[Nit] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2655 — The drift-check rebuild's stack-merge conflict branch (`|| fail <parent>`) and the multi-parent path have no executed test. Only the rebase-conflict branch of `fail()` is exercised. [lanes: testing]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: tests/super-code/test-setup-reuse.sh: every drift fixture uses a single parent (`--parents ep.1`) or none; the only failure case, test_conflict_restores_old_head (line 96), conflicts during `git rebase --onto`, so only `fail rebase` runs. The `${c} || fail ${parents[i]}` path (a `git merge --abort` from a detached HEAD followed by `git checkout -q $br`), more than one parent, a reordered parent list and a deleted parent branch have no executed test. Design line 43 requires restore on \"any conflict\". All three seats judged the shared `fail()` body correct by inspection; the gap is coverage only, in a path that runs after the clean-worktree check.\n  fix-shape hint: add one fixture where a parent's merge into the detached integration tip conflicts and one with two parents; assert the branch is restored to the old head in both."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":10,"key":"[Nit] tests/super-code/test-setup-reuse.sh:26","text":"[Nit] tests/super-code/test-setup-reuse.sh:26 — tests/super-code/test-setup-reuse.sh takes its git author/committer identity from the ambient global/system git config for the commits it creates without the fixture ID: the setUp stack merge, the land merge in test_merged_branch_only_is_already_merged, and the stack merges and `git rebase --onto` inside the emitted drift-check script. With no configured identity, all 7 tests error. [lanes: test-isolation-environment]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: All three seats reproduced at head: `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=<file with user.useConfigOnly=true> GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 bash tests/super-code/test-setup-reuse.sh` gives `Ran 7 tests ... FAILED (errors=7)`; setUp fails at `git merge --no-ff -q -m 'stack: ep.1' task-ep.1` with exit 128. The `ID` flags (line 11) are applied only in `commit()` (line 35); the setUp merge (line 26), the land merge (line 112) and the merges and rebase inside the emitted drift script carry no identity. Sibling tests (test-setup-cleanup.sh, test-task-surface.sh, test-run-marker.sh) set identity explicitly. The file is new in this diff. Only hosts with no git identity (a bare CI runner) are affected.\n  fix-shape hint: set GIT_AUTHOR_NAME/EMAIL and GIT_COMMITTER_NAME/EMAIL in the subprocess env used for every git call and for the drift script run."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":11,"key":"[Nit] tests/super-auto/review-inputs-range-scope.sh:16","text":"[Nit] tests/super-auto/review-inputs-range-scope.sh:16 — None of the three new test files (review-inputs-range-scope.sh, review-continuity-e2e.sh, test-setup-reuse.sh) pins commit.gpgsign=false or core.hooksPath. An ambient commit-signing or global-hooks config therefore reaches every fixture commit, and with signing enabled and no usable signer all three abort. Existing tests in the same suite already pin these settings. [lanes: test-isolation-environment]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `g()` at review-inputs-range-scope.sh:16 and review-continuity-e2e.sh:28, and `ID` at test-setup-reuse.sh:11, set only user.name and user.email. The scout observed exit 128 (`error: gpg failed to sign the data / fatal: failed to write commit object`) for all three under a global `commit.gpgsign=true, gpg.program=false`; review-inputs-range-scope.sh is also called from tests/super-auto/test-scripts.sh:13 under `set -e`, so the failure aborts that suite before its own tests. Existing pins: test-scripts.sh:224, test-run-marker.sh:211, test-sdd-workspace.sh:133. The refute seat dissented: only 3 of about 15 committing test files pin these, so it is not a repo convention, and the user's own config does not trigger it. The reproduce seat conceded the convention is thin but confirmed the exposure; neither confirming seat re-ran the signing reproduction. The core.hooksPath half is unobserved.\n  fix-shape hint: add `-c commit.gpgsign=false -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` to the three helpers."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":12,"key":"[FYI] skills/super-code/scripts/dispatch-text.mjs:348; skills/super-code/scripts/dispatch-text.mjs:349","text":"[FYI] skills/super-code/scripts/dispatch-text.mjs:348; skills/super-code/scripts/dispatch-text.mjs:349 — review-inputs still spawns one `git cat-file` and hashes every archived file of every run at both endpoints, including runs the new relevance rule then classifies as out of range, so wall time grows with total archive history. [lanes: premortem, hot-path-perf]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: pre-existing on base: skills/super-code/scripts/dispatch-text.mjs:275 at 444f86b2 has the same `try { if (hash(blob(p)) !== sha256) collectionGap(...) }` loop; `blob` is one `execFileSync git cat-file` per file. At head the relevance skip `if (!buffering && !relevant(run)) continue` (line ~291; the finding's 348-352 numbering does not match the file) comes after the hash, so out-of-range runs are still read and hashed. The scout measured 13.5s at head versus 18.4s at base on this range, so the diff slightly reduced the cost. All three seats mark it pre-existing; final severity FYI per rule (not introduced by this change; lanes premortem and hot-path-perf).\n  fix-shape hint: hash only for relevant runs, or batch with `git cat-file --batch`; a follow-up, not for this change."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":1,"key":"[FYI] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:145","text":"[FYI] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:145 — `report-verdict --complete` (and strictDraft) accept an ATX sub-heading after items in a section opened by a bold label, silently dropping every item under it with exit 0.\n  reason: 3 REJECT. All three seats reproduced the behavior and cite design §4.1(d): \"A section opened by a bold label is ended by any ATX heading or recognized bold label.\" The code's `level === 0 || lvl <= level` implements that rule; items after `### detail` fall into an unrecognized section by design, as after `## Analysis`. Base gives the same output. At most a test-coverage nit."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":2,"key":"[FYI] skills/super-auto/scripts/scope-dispositions:217; skills/super-auto/scripts/scope-dispositions:170","text":"[FYI] skills/super-auto/scripts/scope-dispositions:217; skills/super-auto/scripts/scope-dispositions:170 — The new scope-filter bookkeeping check applies retroactively to run.md files written under the previous contract, turning previously clean runs into exit 3 with no migration path.\n  reason: 3 REJECT. The reproduction used finished historical run.md archives, not a live run. Exit 3 is \"retain known keys plus explicit gaps\" and is recoverable by appending the missing records from the durable roast reports. The finding itself conceded that fail-loud is the stated intent."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":3,"key":"[FYI] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:3014; skills/super-code/coordinator.js:3015","text":"[FYI] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:3014; skills/super-code/coordinator.js:3015 — The reviewFileFormat self-repair instruction contradicts the write guard in the same prompt, leaving reviewers no sanctioned way to fix formatting of their own file.\n  reason: 2 REJECT, 1 CONFIRM (Nit). reviewWriteGuard is prefixed \"BEFORE ANY ARTIFACT WRITE\" and protects a prior attempt's partial bytes; design §4.1(c) prescribes in-place self-repair for final, cluster and seam reviewers and the sibling `-format-repair.md` only for the super-roast re-dispatch. The finding's own sweep of all 2431 committed review artifacts found none that pass on base and fail on head. In the case pattern `*\" $x \"*` the variable sits inside double quotes, so it is literal, not a glob. The unquoted `$(...)` pathname-expands a bare `*` into worktree filenames, which never match the parent list and set drift=1: the safe direction. Subjects are written by the coordinator's own merge command."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":7,"key":"[FYI] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2644","text":"[FYI] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2644 — Restart re-dispatch of an existing task worktree now runs a drift-check that can rewrite the task branch, with dirty trees or conflicts becoming terminal SETUP_FAILED and no kill switch.\n  reason: 3 REJECT. Design line 43 specifies exactly this: dirty tree with drift → `SETUP_FAILED: prerequisite drift; uncommitted work preserved; nothing reset`; clean tree → detach, re-merge, `rebase --onto`; conflict → restore old head and SETUP_FAILED. The no-drift path still returns REUSE with the old wording. A kill switch is configurability the spec and the repo's guidance rule out."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":8,"key":"[FYI] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2660; skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2658","text":"[FYI] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2660; skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2658 — After a REBUILT rebase nothing keeps a ref to the old head and the coordinator writes no ledger or RESULT trace.\n  reason: 3 REJECT. The spec requires printing the old head and restoring it on conflict, and says \"no new result status\"; it asks for no backup ref, ledger line or RESULT field. The reflog keeps the old head for the default expiry window and task worktrees are rebuilt right after a restart. The phrase \"stays reachable\" is loose wording at most."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":9,"key":"[FYI] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2645","text":"[FYI] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2645 — The drift-check re-resolves `$integ` at three points, so the drift decision and the rebuild base can use different integration tips.\n  reason: 3 REJECT. - finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"roast-code/round-2","artifact":"2026-10-28-review-continuity-followups-roast-pr-2.md","kind":"roast","verdict":"Should-fix (3 confirmed) [low coverage] [review readiness incomplete]","occurrences":[{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":1,"key":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:226","text":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:226 — The new `markable()` guard in `report-verdict --repair-check` still lets a repair put `- ` in front of a code-fence delimiter line (``` or ~~~). That dissolves the fence, so a `## ` heading inside the fence becomes a real section boundary in the repair. A Confirmed finding is reclassified into `Rejected (with reason)` and the repair is accepted with exit 0. (new; same defect class as the prior :223/:229 demotion finding, different trigger) [lanes: correctness] [fix-regression]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: All three seats reproduced at head 9b358f04 with the head script copied to the scratchpad. Original: `## Confirmed findings` opens with a fenced block that contains `## Rejected (with reason)`, followed by `- [Should-fix] a.sh:1 — real finding`, then a real `## Rejected (with reason)` with `- [Nit] b.sh:2 — refuted`. `--complete --source x o.md` exits 4 with \"finding text without a list marker in Confirmed findings\", pointing at the fence opener, so the original is dispatched for repair. Repair: `sed 's/^```$/- ```/'`, which applies the error's own hint to both delimiters. `--complete` on the repair exits 0 with occurrences Confirmed #1 key \"```\", Rejected #1 key \"```\", Rejected #2 `[Should-fix] a.sh:1`, Rejected #3 `[Nit] b.sh:2`. `--repair-check o.md r.md` exits 0. Cause: `markable` at line 226 excludes list items, `#` headings and bold-led lines but not fence delimiters; the `--complete` fence regex `^ {0,3}(`{3,}|~{3,})` at line 140 does not match `- ```` `, so the fence dissolves. Check (iii) misses it because `keys_roast` (awk at ~line 368) has no fence tracking and ends the section at the fenced `## Rejected` in both files, giving identical empty key lists. Design §4.1(c) says lines inside a fence \"compare as plain lines\" and \"every original line keeps its section because no section boundary changes\". The trigger is narrow (a fence containing a `## ` line, with no preceding item in the section), but `--complete`'s own error message suggests the exact repair that triggers it. The misfiled occurrence feeds the `finding-source` record that scope-dispositions reads.\n  fix-shape hint: add `!/^ {0,3}(?:`{3,}|~{3,})/.test(a)` to `markable`, and add a rejection test with a fenced `##` line inside a finding section."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":2,"key":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:226","text":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:226 — The `markable` guard in `--repair-check` rule (iv) still accepts a `- ` marker on an unindented (lazy) continuation line of an existing finding, so an accepted repair splits one finding into two `--complete` occurrences; the new r11 test covers only the indented case. (regressed: prior [Should-fix] report-verdict:229 from iteration 1 was fixed incompletely) [lanes: regression, testing] [fix-regression]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: All three seats reproduced at head 9b358f04. Reproduce and refute seats: original with `## Confirmed findings` / `- [Should-fix] src/a.sh:3 — the call drops the error.` / unindented `Evidence: the exit code is never read.` / `## Rejected (with reason)` / `- none`; repair via `sed 's/^Evidence:/- Evidence:/'`. `--repair-check o.md r.md` exits 0. `--complete` on the original gives 1 occurrence whose text includes the Evidence line; on the repair it gives 2 occurrences, position 1 without its evidence and position 2 keyed `Evidence: the exit code is never read.`. Ground seat got the same by un-indenting the `evidence:` line of tests/super-code/fixtures/report-verdict/roast-template-shaped.md; the spurious position 2 also absorbs the fix-shape hint. Cause: line 226 `markable = a => /^\\S/.test(a) && !item.test(a) && ...` decides by indentation alone, but `--complete` (lines ~158-166, `else if (item) item.lines.push(line)`) treats any non-bullet line after an open item as continuation, indented or not. Check (iii) cannot see the change because roast `--keys` reads only `- [SEV]` lines. The fix commit 4805721d applied the prior fix-shape hint (\"no leading whitespace\") literally; the only new test, tests/super-code/test-report-verdict.sh:633 (r11), uses an indented line. Design §4.1(c): a repair \"cannot ... move a continuation line\". The prior hint was too narrow; the correct condition is the parser's, not indentation.\n  fix-shape hint: decide markability from the original's parse state: allow the `- ` prefix only on a line that `--complete` would report as unmarked text, i.e. one with no open item before it in the same section; add an unindented-continuation rejection test."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":3,"key":"[Nit] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:226","text":"[Nit] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:226 — The `markable` guard rejects adding a `- ` marker to any unindented line that starts with bold text, including bold-led finding or prose lines that are not section labels (e.g. `**[Nit] src/a.sh:3** — claim`, `**Note:** text`). Neither `markable` nor `demoted()` covers such a line, so no repair can pass, which is stricter than design §4.1(c)(iv). (new) [lanes: api-contract] [fix-regression]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: All three seats confirmed by reading head 9b358f04; none re-ran the scout's reproduction. The last `markable` clause `!/^\\*\\*[^*]+\\*\\*/` matches any line that begins with a bold span, not only a whole-line label. `demoted()` matches only `^\\*\\*([^*]+)\\*\\*[ \\t]*$` and emits `- <label>`, dropping trailing text. So for `**[Nit] src/a.sh:3** — claim` the content-preserving repair `- **[Nit] ...** — claim` falls through every branch to `reject('(iv) line N differs')`. The scout observed `--repair-check` exit 0 at 5e427d04 and exit 4 (\"(iv) line 21 differs\") at head for that shape, and the same for `**Note:** the spot check ran once`. Design §4.1(c)(iv), unchanged at head, allows a repair line that \"is that line prefixed with `- ` (the original line was not already a list item)\" and restricts only label-only demotion to non-roast-word labels. The guard has a rationale (the extractor's `boundary()` treats any bold-led line as a section boundary, so prefixing `**Findings**` would erase one), but it is broader than that need. Failure is fail-safe: the original stays the round's report path and collection records \"complete extraction unavailable\". No test covers a bold-led non-label line.\n  fix-shape hint: narrow the bold clause to whole-line labels or recognized roast-word labels, or amend §4.1(c)(iv) to state the exclusion; add one test either way."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":1,"key":"[FYI] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:173","text":"[FYI] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:173 — The new `unclosed code fence` grammar error (exit 4) is one the super-roast repair path can never accept, because rule (iv) rejects adding or removing a fence line; a report with an unclosed or mismatched fence costs one wasted reporter re-dispatch, the original stands, and the round gets no `finding-route` records.\n  reason: 3 REJECT. The mechanics are accurate (line 173 raises the error; rule (iv) requires equal line counts, so adding or removing an indented fence line fails with \"(iv) line count differs\"). All three seats cite design §4.1(c): the repair \"can only add list markers and `- none`, demote a forbidden sub-heading to an item, and drop blank lines; it cannot drop, reword, reorder or relocate a line\", and \"A shape that needs any other edit ... is rejected loudly rather than checked weakly.\" The fallback is also specified: \"the original is the round's report path ... and collection's existing `complete extraction unavailable` gap stands\", and super-auto SKILL.md:399 describes the unrouted-gap projection. Before the fix the same input silently misfiled findings (prior iteration-1 Should-fix at :140); now it fails loudly with a visible gap, bounded to one re-dispatch. Widening (iv) to allow fence edits would contradict the spec's deliberate safety property. The finding states it was not observed in a live run."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":1,"key":"[Nit] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2656","text":"[Nit] skills/super-code/coordinator.js:2656 — The fix pass (c1e7fab1) stopped the drift-check rebuild from using the shared `stackMerges` helper and hand-copied `git merge --no-ff -m \"stack: <parent>\"` inline, so the fresh-cut path and the drift rebuild each define the stack-merge subject that the drift check (lines 2646, 2650) and the reviewer BASE CHECK parse back. (spot refute: confirmed Nit; design line 83 says one local helper serves both paths; the two copies produce identical text today, so this is a latent maintenance risk, not a present defect; the replay-harness.mjs:897 literal-count comment is also stale)"},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":2,"key":"[FYI] skills/super-code/coordinator-workflow.md:1173","text":"[FYI] skills/super-code/coordinator-workflow.md:1173 — The round-1 drift-check fix changed three things (inline SHA merges instead of `stackMerges`, skipping a parent whose ref is gone, a new \"restore not verified\" outcome) without updating coordinator-workflow.md:1173-1176 or the design's component table. (spot refute: rejected; the inline merge still produces the same `stack: <parent>` commits the doc describes, the skipped parent is a refinement of the doc's own \"not an ancestor\" rule, the new outcome is still `SETUP_FAILED` naming the old head, and the design doc is a point-in-time run artifact; a documentation suggestion, not a gap)"}]}

- finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"roast-design/round-1","artifact":"2026-10-28-review-continuity-followups-roast-design-1.md","kind":"roast","verdict":"Should-fix (9 confirmed)","occurrences":[{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":1,"key":"[Should-fix] §4.1(d) \"a sub-heading or bold-label line encountered inside a recognized section that already has items is an error (exit 2), not a section end\"","text":"[Should-fix] §4.1(d) \"a sub-heading or bold-label line encountered inside a recognized section that already has items is an error (exit 2), not a section end\" — The spec never defines what distinguishes a forbidden sub-heading or bold-label line from the heading that starts the next sibling section. Read literally the rule rejects canonical back-to-back-section reports and bold label lines; read narrowly the implementer must invent the discriminator.\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict `heading()` L90-93 matches `^#+[ \\t]+(.*)` or `^\\*\\*([^*]+)\\*\\*`, discards heading level, and treats a bold label exactly like an ATX heading; every match calls `closeSection()` (L122-128). §4.1(a) keeps recognized sections back to back (`## Must fix before landing` → `## Untested scope` → `## Deferred OK`; roast `## Confirmed findings` → `## Escalations`) and sends narrative under non-recognized headings (`## Findings` then `## Analysis`). No spec sentence names heading depth, a recognized-word test, or a bold-label rule. Both grammar copies and the shared fixtures (§4.1(d), Key abstractions L87, L95) must agree on one discriminator.\n  fix-shape hint: One sentence in §4.1(d): an ATX heading deeper than the section's own opening level is a sub-heading; a heading using a recognized section word at any level is a sibling; a bold-label line is an error only when it is not itself a recognized section label."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":2,"key":"[Should-fix] §4.1(c) \"re-dispatches the reporter once with the parser error and the original path, writing the repaired report to a new sibling file ... The repair path is the one recorded in the round's report list\"","text":"[Should-fix] §4.1(c) \"re-dispatches the reporter once with the parser error and the original path, writing the repaired report to a new sibling file ... The repair path is the one recorded in the round's report list\" — The format repair has an LLM reporter rewrite the whole report and records the repair as the round's report, but only parse success is checked. Nothing compares findings, keys, verdict or header qualifiers between original and repair, so a dropped or altered finding passes silently into collection and convergence.\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived — refute seat voted REJECT, outvoted / ground ✓)\n  evidence: §4.1(c) L27: the only content guard is \"the finding content is never edited by the orchestrator\", which binds the orchestrator, not the re-dispatched reporter. The only acceptance test is `--complete` exit 0. Grep of the spec for compar/occurrence/unchanged/dropped/identical/diff finds no original-versus-repair check. Collection, the punch list and convergence read only the recorded repair path; the kept original is read by nothing. This contradicts Goal \"extract completely or fail loudly\" and §2 \"extraction must not start ignoring narrative findings\". Refute seat's point that the spec defers a \"mechanical post-dispatch repair with report-status pairing\" was answered by the reproduce seat: that is a different mechanism and does not accept this gap.\n  fix-shape hint: Require identical `--complete` occurrence key lists and identical header lines for original and repair; on any mismatch fall back to the original, keep the existing extraction gap, and log a friction line."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":3,"key":"[Should-fix] §4.1(c) \"final/cluster/seam reviewers run `report-verdict --complete <their file>` before returning\"","text":"[Should-fix] §4.1(c) \"final/cluster/seam reviewers run `report-verdict --complete <their file>` before returning\" — The self-check and orchestrator invocations omit the required `--source`, and the repair dispatch is keyed on exit 2 without reserving exit 2 for grammar failures, so a literal rendering fails or misroutes a usage error as a format repair.\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict L43-53: usage is `report-verdict --complete --source IMMUTABLE_SOURCE_ID FILE`; missing `--source` prints \"requires --source\" and exits 2. Grammar failures set exitCode 2 at L150-151; unreadable-file path also exits 2. The spec writes `report-verdict --complete <their file>` three times with no source id, and a grep of the spec finds no `--source` and no exit-code distinction. §4.1(c) routes every orchestrator exit 2 to a reporter re-dispatch and a \"format repair\" friction line.\n  severity note: reproduce seat Should-fix; refute and ground seats Nit on the expectation that rendered-fixture tests catch a missing `--source`. Lifted to Should-fix because §4.1(c) keys the repair dispatch on exit 2 and all three seats agree exit 2 is shared by usage, I/O and grammar failures; which signal the orchestrator keys on is a design decision the spec must state, not an implementation detail tests can settle.\n  fix-shape hint: Name the exact invocation including a fixed self-check source id; separate grammar failure from usage or I/O failure by a distinct exit code or stderr prefix; have the orchestrator re-dispatch only on grammar failure."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":4,"key":"[Should-fix] §4.5 \"Branch-only or worktree-only states return a typed SETUP_FAILED naming which exists, preserving both\"","text":"[Should-fix] §4.5 \"Branch-only or worktree-only states return a typed SETUP_FAILED naming which exists, preserving both\" — Making branch-only a hard SETUP_FAILED collides with a normal post-merge state (worktree removed with `--keep-branch` when a review bead exists). The spec does not order the new check against the ALREADY-MERGED CHECK or say whether it applies to the re-entry path.\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived — refute seat voted REJECT, outvoted / ground ✓)\n  evidence: coordinator.js:2860 and :2912 pass `--keep-branch` to remove-task-worktree when a review bead exists, so a landed task is routinely branch-only. Existing workspaceSetup text (coordinator.js:2638-2644) handles only NEITHER and BOTH; the ALREADY-MERGED CHECK runs inside NEITHER or afterward. The spec (§4.5 L43, Key abstractions L83) names no ordering and no re-entry scope. Refute seat's reachability evidence (bd close precedes worktree removal at L2912; lost-close handled by closeAlreadyMerged L1433/L1812 outside workspaceSetup) narrows the window but the seat itself concedes an ordering clarification is warranted; a restart after a crash between merge and close still reaches the fragment.\n  fix-shape hint: One clause: run the already-merged check before the branch-only/worktree-only failure, and state whether the new fragment applies to the review re-entry path."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":5,"key":"[Should-fix] Key abstractions \"Range relevance rule: built once per invocation from the captured `changed` list and `current`\" vs Owns \"or when it is re-read as a mirror origin\"; Run-scoped gap sink","text":"[Should-fix] Key abstractions \"Range relevance rule: built once per invocation from the captured `changed` list and `current`\" vs Owns \"or when it is re-read as a mirror origin\"; Run-scoped gap sink — The relevance rule cannot be built once up front if one criterion is mirror-origin status, which is learned during origin authentication after snapshot and collection problems may already have been routed to non-blocking `outOfRange`. No evaluation order or reclassification is specified.\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived — refute seat voted REJECT, outvoted / ground ✓)\n  evidence: Spec L59-60 \"built once per invocation\"; §4.2 L31 \"strict as today\" and \"Relevant runs keep every existing guard\"; sink L65-69 routes each problem at call time; no two-phase or reclassification step anywhere in the spec. skills/super-code/scripts/dispatch-text.mjs: `snapshotGap` emitted during snapshot parsing L153-214; `authenticateOrigins` runs at L362-363 and calls `validateCollections` at L332, which emits `collectionGap` for the origin run. A mirror-origin run's problems are therefore filed before the run is known to be an origin. Refute seat read \"(strict as today)\" as an existing strict path but did not check the code order.\n  fix-shape hint: State the order: resolve origins first, or buffer run-attributed problems and classify them after `authenticateOrigins` resolves mirror origins."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":6,"key":"[Should-fix] §4.7 (task reviewer)","text":"[Should-fix] §4.7 (task reviewer) — The reviewer is told to grade project commit-policy violations as Important but is not told to read the repository instruction files (AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, CONTRIBUTING) that define the policy, so it can detect only constraints that happen to be in the brief.\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✗ — seat voted REJECT / refute ✗-survived — spot check CONFIRMed, promoted / ground ✓)\n  evidence: §4.7 points the implementer at the instruction files and the brief's Global Constraints; for the reviewer it adds only the severity rule and `git log --format=%B BASE..HEAD`. Grep of the spec for AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, CONTRIBUTING, \"instruction files\", \"commit rules\" finds no reviewer-side read. skills/super-code/task-reviewer-prompt.md \"## Read\" lists only the brief, the report and the diff, and permits other files only \"to check a concrete risk you can name\". §1 states Claude Code does not auto-load AGENTS.md. Reproduce seat's point (brief carries Global Constraints) is answered by the majority: the spec does not guarantee the planner copies an AGENTS.md-only policy into the brief.\n  fix-shape hint: One clause in the reviewer prompt: check commit messages against the same instruction files plus the brief's Global Constraints."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":7,"key":"[Nit] §4.7 \"a violation of an explicit project or brief constraint","text":"[Nit] §4.7 \"a violation of an explicit project or brief constraint — commit-message policy included — is Important ... no history rewrite\" — An already-committed bad message cannot be cleared by a forward-only fix pass, and the spec names no remedy (amend allowance or accepted disposition).\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✗ — seat voted REJECT / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: Spec L51 grades the violation Important and lists \"no history rewrite\" beside \"no metadata-lint framework\". Grep for rewrite/amend/reword/trailer finds no remedy. Both confirming seats concede the reproduce seat's point that \"no history rewrite\" most plausibly means no new rewrite machinery, and that implementer-prompt.md L194 limits the loop to one fix pass, so the claimed cycle to a cap does not occur. What remains is a wording ambiguity about what the fix pass may do with its own unmerged commits.\n  fix-shape hint: One sentence: the fix pass may reword the task's own unmerged commits, or the reviewer records a forward-only disposition for an already-bad message."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":8,"key":"[Nit] §4.6 \"A parity test runs a list of ids through both resolvers and requires equal windows\"; Variation \"parity test catches drift\"","text":"[Nit] §4.6 \"A parity test runs a list of ids through both resolvers and requires equal windows\"; Variation \"parity test catches drift\" — A hand-kept id list cannot detect a row added to `CLAUDE_WINDOWS` alone, so the D5 drift the test is claimed to catch recurs for the next model.\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived — refute seat voted REJECT, outvoted / ground ✓)\n  evidence: Spec L47, L75, L93, L101: the test \"runs a list of ids\"; nothing derives the list from the table rows or compares row counts. A new 1M row in context-pass.mjs with no matching id leaves both resolvers agreeing on every listed id; context-preflight falls through to 200K, which is the 1s3/D5 defect. Refute seat's point (deliberate hand-kept mirror, cross-reference comments) explains the design but does not make \"catches drift\" true for an unlisted row.\n  fix-shape hint: Require one positive and one boundary-negative id per `CLAUDE_WINDOWS` row plus a row-count or regex-set check, or soften the \"catches drift\" claim at L93."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":9,"key":"[Nit] §4.4 \"A matching roast occurrence is a finding-source occurrence from the same round's roast source whose extracted key equals the recorded scope-filter key\"","text":"[Nit] §4.4 \"A matching roast occurrence is a finding-source occurrence from the same round's roast source whose extracted key equals the recorded scope-filter key\" — The spec does not name the roast source-id convention it relies on, nor which path (original or `…-format-repair.md`) the finding-source must cite.\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✗ — seat voted REJECT)\n  evidence: Demoted from the seats' Should-fix on the ground seat's evidence: skills/super-auto/SKILL.md:511 already defines the roast source as `roast-design/round-N` / `roast-code/round-N`, which carries both kind and round, and all three seats on the design-roast finding-source packet cite the same line. That disproves the majority's premise that roast reports have no source-id convention. What survives is that §4.4 does not cite the convention and does not say the finding-source `artifact` is the recorded path (repair when one passes `--complete`, else original).\n  fix-shape hint: Cite the existing `roast-code/round-N` / `roast-design/round-N` convention in §4.4 and state that the finding-source artifact equals the path recorded in `roast-code:`/`roast-design:`."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":1,"key":"§4.1(d) \"already has items\" qualifier","text":"§4.1(d) \"already has items\" qualifier — Rejected 3/3. report-verdict `closeSection()` already exits 2 on an empty recognized section (\"empty finding section\"), and assembly-archive.cjs L24 has the same guard; `## Findings` followed directly by `### Critical` fails today, so the qualifier covers the only remaining silent case."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":2,"key":"§4.5 `git rebase --onto` drops merge commits","text":"§4.5 `git rebase --onto` drops merge commits — Rejected 3/3. oldBase is the newest first-parent `stack:` commit, so every spec-created merge sits at or below it; no spec step has an implementer or fix pass create a merge above it. - finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"roast-design/round-2","artifact":"2026-10-28-review-continuity-followups-roast-design-2.md","kind":"roast","verdict":"Should-fix (6 confirmed) [converged]","occurrences":[{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":1,"key":"[Should-fix] §4.2 \"or it is re-read as a mirror origin (strict as today)\" / \"Every run named as the origin of a mirror declaration that `authenticateOrigins` examined counts as re-read, whether or not the declaration authenticated\"; Key abstractions, Range relevance rule","text":"[Should-fix] §4.2 \"or it is re-read as a mirror origin (strict as today)\" / \"Every run named as the origin of a mirror declaration that `authenticateOrigins` examined counts as re-read, whether or not the declaration authenticated\"; Key abstractions, Range relevance rule — The round-1 fix makes the origin of every mirror declaration in the tree relevant, whether or not the mirror run is in range, so an untouched old mirror/origin pair still blocks a product-only range, which is the §1 rsc defect the section sets out to fix. (new; introduced by the round-1 fix of the prior \"Range relevance rule built once\" finding, which is itself resolved)\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: skills/super-code/scripts/dispatch-text.mjs `authenticateOrigins` (~L296-350) builds `declared` from every `*.collection-origin.json` in the endpoint tree, not from changed paths, and runs unconditionally for both endpoints (L362-363). §4.2 keeps all runs parsed and never limits which declarations are examined. Under the new clause every examined origin counts as re-read and relevant, and relevant runs \"keep every existing guard (parse problems, incomplete claimed collections, origin authentication)\". An untouched mirror M with untouched origin O therefore sends O's `validateCollections` and re-snapshot problems to blocking `gaps`, while M's own gap goes to `outOfRange`. This contradicts the Goal, §1 and the Walk-through (\"old run irrelevant ... exit 0\"). investigation/inv-rsc.md L62 took the narrower route (\"filter `declared` ... by `touches(mirrorRun)`\"), so its passing suites validated a different rule. The ambiguity check does not need origin relevance, since all runs are parsed regardless. Reproduce seat ran it against the worktree script: original exits 4 (sub-heading inside Confirmed findings); the demoted repair fails `--repair-check` with exit 4, \"(iii) --keys output differs\". The original then stays the round's report path and the `complete extraction unavailable` gap stands, after one wasted reporter re-dispatch and a friction line. The spec's \"Unchanged behavior\" bullet (L108) keeps (iii) and says demotions \"stay accepted\", which is false for this section. (iv) keeps every other line byte-identical, so any `--keys` difference can only come from (iv)-permitted edits; (iii) therefore adds nothing except this rejection. The spec carves out header-only failures (\"check (ii) can never pass for them\") but names no matching exclusion for (iii). Existing fixtures (tests/super-code/test-report-verdict.sh L544-648) exercise repairs only under Rejected/Unverified nits/Escalations, never Confirmed findings. Not Blocking: the fallback is safe and nothing is silently lost. Companion to the next finding: together they mean the Confirmed-section repair either rejects the canonical form or accepts an unkeyed one.\n  fix-shape hint: Drop (iii) as subsumed by (iv), or let (iii) tolerate keys gained only from lines (iv) marked or demoted; add a Confirmed-findings fixture for each of the three shapes (unmarked prefix, `###` demotion, bold-led prefix)."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":2,"key":"[Should-fix] §4.1(c) rule (iv) \"bold-led non-label prose such as `**Note:** text` or `**[Nit] a.sh:3**","text":"[Should-fix] §4.1(c) rule (iv) \"bold-led non-label prose such as `**Note:** text` or `**[Nit] a.sh:3** — claim` inside a recognized section is a `sub-heading` line and may take a `- ` prefix\"; check (iii); §4.4 \"scope-filter decision without finding-route\" — The spec sanctions a repair that turns a malformed Confirmed finding (`**[SEV] x** — claim`) into an occurrence that `--complete` extracts but `--keys` never lists, while (iii) forbids the canonical `- [SEV] x` fix. The accepted repair clears the loud `complete extraction unavailable` gap, but the finding gets no scope-filter decision, no `finding-route`, no punch-list entry, and the new §4.4 gap does not fire for it. Drift with a dirty worktree (ignoring `.tmp/`) → `SETUP_FAILED: prerequisite drift; uncommitted work preserved; nothing reset`\"; Key abstractions `workspaceSetup` \"Every failure is `SETUP_FAILED` ... the existing mapping to BLOCKED carries it\" — The second drift trigger also fires when the parent has simply landed. Combined with the dirty-worktree rule, a restarted task holding uncommitted work ends in a terminal SETUP_FAILED → BLOCKED where today the same restart resumes. The merge-time rebase already handles a landed parent, so this case needs no reconciliation. (new) [lanes: domain:git-workspace-management]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: skills/super-code/coordinator.js L1639/L1652 build `parents` as `effDeps(row).filter(d => implDone.has(d))`, and L1647 says parents are \"implemented, split, not yet merged\". Once parent p lands it leaves `parents`. The child's own `stack: p` merge commit was never merged into integration, so it stays in `integ..br`. The implemented check (~L2650) loops `git log --first-parent --grep='^stack: ' $integ..$br` and sets drift=1 for any X not in `parents`, so a landed p counts as drift. With a dirty worktree the script prints SETUP_FAILED and exits 1; the emitted `driftText` (~L2661) says \"do not retry, reset or recut\"; §2 forbids WIP-commit and stash, so the run has no recovery step. The both-exist reuse text (L2665) expects uncommitted work (\"an earlier attempt's committed or uncommitted work is where you continue from\"), so a dirty tree at restart is routine, and a parent landing during a long run is routine. Merge-time `rebase --onto` from the stack base (~L2860 comment) already drops the parents' pre-review commits. The v94 defect is trigger 1 (a current parent's code missing from the branch); trigger 2 widens the hard fail beyond it. The spec never separates a landed parent from a removed dependency (grep for landed, merged, \"no longer\" finds only §4.5 L43 and L97). Work is preserved and a human can finish the task, so Should-fix rather than Blocking.\n  fix-shape hint: Exclude from the stale-`stack:` trigger any X whose branch is already merged into the integration branch (treat it as no drift), or limit the dirty-worktree hard fail to the missing-ancestor trigger."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":4,"key":"[Should-fix] §4.1(a) \"each recognized section holds only `- ` items (indented continuation lines allowed)\" vs §4.1(c) role list \"`continuation` (any non-bullet line after an open item, indented or not)\" and Unchanged behavior \"--complete grammar rules stay as they are\"","text":"[Should-fix] §4.1(a) \"each recognized section holds only `- ` items (indented continuation lines allowed)\" vs §4.1(c) role list \"`continuation` (any non-bullet line after an open item, indented or not)\" and Unchanged behavior \"--complete grammar rules stay as they are\" — The body rule allows only indented continuation lines, but the `--complete` grammar the spec keeps unchanged silently folds an unindented unmarked finding line into the preceding item's text with exit 0, and (iv) classifies that line as `continuation`, which must stay byte-identical. Such a report is neither failed loudly nor repairable. Bounded: intent is recoverable from git history, no behavior is wrong yet.\n  fix-shape hint: One scoping sentence (\"relative to the pre-amend implementation on this branch, as of code-1\") or move the block into amend-code-1.md; also fix the \"(i)-(iii)\" count."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":1,"key":"§4.1(c) \"`--classify` ... runs the `--complete` node block's own section/fence/item state machine ... no second copy\" vs \"`sub-heading` (... whether or not the section has items yet; it does not open a section, so it and the lines after it keep the enclosing section)\"","text":"§4.1(c) \"`--classify` ... runs the `--complete` node block's own section/fence/item state machine ... no second copy\" vs \"`sub-heading` (... whether or not the section has items yet; it does not open a section, so it and the lines after it keep the enclosing section)\" — Rejected 2/3 (reproduce REJECT, refute REJECT, ground CONFIRM at Should-fix). All three seats agree on the mechanism: `--complete` raises the sub-heading error only under `active && hasContent`; an item-less recognized section followed by a deeper heading falls through to closeSection() and opens a new section (report-verdict ~L147-155), so `--complete`'s own transitions would label that line `boundary`, not `sub-heading`. The majority holds that the spec resolves this itself: the role definition explicitly says \"whether or not the section has items yet\", a specific definition that controls over the general \"own state machine\" wording; \"no second copy\" means shared `heading()`/`relevant()`/fence/bullet code, and a classify-mode flag on one heading branch is not a second copy; §4.1(d) leaves `--complete`'s transitions alone, so `--complete` output stays byte-identical as the mlr.21 bead requires; \"Section-body grammar errors do not stop classification\" already signals that `--classify` need not mirror `--complete` on erroring input. Ground seat's dissent cites the same code and spec text and concedes the mode-flag resolution; it adds nothing the majority does not address, so no escalation. Residual: a wording nit — say that classify mode may differ from `--complete` at item-less sub-headings."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":2,"key":"§4.1(c) rule (iv) checks only per-line `(section, inFence)` and byte identity, not item membership, so prefixing `- ` to a sub-heading or bold label after an open item splits the finding and moves its continuation lines onto a new occurrence","text":"§4.1(c) rule (iv) checks only per-line `(section, inFence)` and byte identity, not item membership, so prefixing `- ` to a sub-heading or bold label after an open item splits the finding and moves its continuation lines onto a new occurrence — Rejected 2/3 (reproduce CONFIRM at Nit, refute REJECT, ground REJECT). Mechanism agreed by all seats: demoting `### Evidence` to `- Evidence` after `- [Should-fix] a.sh:3 — claim` passes (i)-(iv), and the `verdict:`/`evidence:` lines then belong to the new severity-less item. The majority holds this is the spec's stated behavior: \"the repair can only add list markers and `- none`, demote a forbidden sub-heading to an item\", a demoted sub-heading is by construction a new item, the existing fixture (tests/super-code/test-report-verdict.sh L558-562, `### detail` → `- detail`) accepts exactly this shape and the spec says such repairs \"stay accepted\". \"Cannot ... move a continuation line\" refers to position, order and section, which (iv) checks byte-for-byte; item membership is not a stated guarantee. Finding keys, severities and every line of text are preserved; the original was already a failed parse with exit 4. The proposed fix (forbid the prefix whenever an item is open) would reject the main case the repair exists for (§1, §4.1(d)). Reproduce seat's Nit rests on the same facts and asks only for softer wording, so no escalation. Residual wording nit: soften the closing sentence of (c) to say grouping may change when a sub-heading is demoted. This is the only residue of the iteration-2 \"checks (ii)-(iv) blind to continuation lines\" finding, which the amended (iv) otherwise resolves."}]}

- finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"roast-code/round-3","artifact":"2026-10-28-review-continuity-followups-roast-pr-3.md","kind":"roast","verdict":"Nit (2 confirmed) [low coverage] [review readiness incomplete]","occurrences":[{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":1,"key":"[Nit] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:253","text":"[Nit] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:253 — Commit a6f5953a changed `--repair-check` rule (iii) to `process.exit(0)` for a non-roast repair instead of rejecting it, contradicting design §4.1(c) (\"a repair whose kind is not `roast` is rejected\") and the mlr.23 bead; a non-roast repair whose recovered finding gets no `--keys` key is accepted silently, and neither the design nor the coordinator docs were amended to match. (new) [lanes: regression, api-contract] [fix-regression]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: All three seats read head b9918d23 line 253: `if (kind !== 'roast') process.exit(0);`, header comment \"(other kinds skip this rule)\". Commit a6f5953a changed `reject('(iii) not a roast report: ...')` to this exit and flipped tests/super-code/test-report-verdict.sh:795 `rc3 \"final-review repair\"` from expecting rc 4 to rc 0. Design §4.1(c) at head (last edited in ff1ca88d, before a6f5953a) still says a non-roast repair is rejected; amend-code-1.md only says checks (i)-(iii) are kept. Refute seat identified the cause: the mlr.21 cross-copy test (~L761, a final-review pair) collided with the strict wording and the merge widened the code contract instead of fixing the test or amending the spec. Scout reproduction at head: a final-review original with a second `## Must fix (second pass)` section whose repair adds `- ` yields two Must-fix occurrences but `--keys` prints only the first; `--repair-check` exits 0. Severity: two seats Nit, one Should-fix. Settled at Nit on the seat evidence: rule (iv) still runs for non-roast repairs (the `|| exit $?` does not fire on exit 0), so byte-identity and line alignment are still enforced; the only documented caller (skills/super-roast/SKILL.md:236) passes roast reports, so the path is reached only when `--complete` misclassifies a roast-written file; no live run hits it. Profile fact: single-operator internal helper with no external caller of this mode.\n  fix-shape hint: either restore the `reject('(iii) not a roast report')` and move the mlr.21 cross-copy test to a roast pair, or amend design §4.1(c) (and the mlr.23 bead's acceptance) to say rule (iii) applies to roast reports only; one line either way."},{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":2,"key":"[Nit] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:290","text":"[Nit] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:290 — `--repair-check` accepts a repair that marks only the first of two consecutive unmarked confirmed findings; the second becomes a lazy continuation of the first, so the repair passes (i)-(iv) with exit 0 while one stranded Confirmed finding loses its own occurrence and `--keys` key. (new) [lanes: correctness]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: All three seats reproduced at head b9918d23 (refute seat included, before rejecting on spec grounds). Original: verdict line \"(2 confirmed)\", `## Confirmed findings`, two unprefixed lines `[Should-fix] a.sh:1 — first claim` and `[Should-fix] b.sh:2 — second claim`. `--complete` on the original exits 4 with \"finding text without a list marker in Confirmed findings\"; the message names no line. Repair: `- ` on the a.sh line only. `--complete` on the repair exits 0 with ONE occurrence keyed `[Should-fix] a.sh:1` whose text includes the b.sh line; `--keys` prints only `[Should-fix] a.sh:1`; `--classify` shows line 5 role `unmarked` in the original and `continuation` in the repair; `--repair-check o.md r.md` exits 0. Why no check catches it: (iii) compares the repair's `--keys` against the repair's own `--complete` occurrences, both see one finding; (iv) at lines 288-293 compares only text, section and inFence for an unchanged line and never compares roles, and `ok` accepts `b === a` for an `unmarked` original. Design §4.1(c)(iii) exists so that no occurrence goes without \"scope decision, `finding-route` or punch-list entry\", and the design says \"a stranded confirmed finding can be recovered\"; here the recovery is silently partial. Refute dissent (REJECT, FYI): design §4.1(a) explicitly accepts the fold as a residual (\"an unindented finding line written after an item is folded into the preceding item's text — kept verbatim ... but given no key of its own\"), rule (iv) says an unmarked line \"may be prefixed\" and does not require it, and a partial repair is a reporter-quality failure the spec bounds. The ground seat concedes those points and rates Nit; the reproduce seat argues the residual covers producer-written reports, not a repair of an already-rejected report. Severity settled at Nit on the seat evidence: the text is kept verbatim (nothing dropped), the spec's letter permits the fold, and the trigger needs a partial reporter repair; the gap against (iii)'s stated purpose is real, so confirmed rather than rejected. No profile demotion applied.\n  fix-shape hint: in rule (iv), reject when an original `unmarked` line is kept unprefixed and its repair role is `continuation` (or require every original `unmarked` line in a finding section to gain `- `); add a test with two consecutive unmarked confirmed findings where only the first is marked."}]}

- finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"inv-20261028T235807/final","artifact":"reviews/inv-20261028T235807/final-review-inv-20261028T235807.md","kind":"final-review","verdict":"not ready (unreviewed: whole-epic coverage and omission provenance remain incomplete)","occurrences":[{"section":"Findings","position":1,"key":"[Important] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:298 — the mlr.27 rule `ra.role === 'unmarked' && b === a && rb.role === 'continuation'` rejects every marker-only repair of a stranded confirmed finding that has continuation lines, and the reporter template (skills/super-roast/reporter-prompt.md:247-250) gives every confirmed finding three indented continuation lines (verdict:, evidence:, fix-shape hint:). Repro at head: an original whose Confirmed findings body is `[Should-fix] a.sh:1 — first claim (new) [lanes: correctness]` / `  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓)` / `  evidence: seats reproduced` (no `- ` marker) fails --complete with exit 4. The correct repair, `- ` on the first line only, passes --complete with exactly one keyed occurrence, but --repair-check exits 4 with `(iv) line 5 unmarked line becomes a continuation:   verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓)`. Marking every line instead fails (iii). So no marker-only repair can pass. At 487992e1 the same pair was accepted (exit 0). The cause: --classify labels every line after the first unmarked line as `unmarked`, because no item is open, so the indented continuation lines of the stranded finding are indistinguishable from a second stranded finding. Impact: design §4.1(c) says \"a stranded confirmed finding can be recovered\". For the template shape it now never can be, and the round falls back to the loud `complete extraction unavailable` gap. This fails safe (original stands, nothing silently lost), but it disables the feature's main use case. The suite stays green because every stranded-finding fixture (test-report-verdict.sh:719-728, :801-802) is single-line. Fix shape: limit the new rejection to lines that would be keyed findings themselves (e.g. a roast `[SEV] location` lead in Confirmed findings) or to unindented lines, and add a test that repairs a multi-line template-shaped stranded finding.","text":"[Important] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:298 — the mlr.27 rule `ra.role === 'unmarked' && b === a && rb.role === 'continuation'` rejects every marker-only repair of a stranded confirmed finding that has continuation lines, and the reporter template (skills/super-roast/reporter-prompt.md:247-250) gives every confirmed finding three indented continuation lines (verdict:, evidence:, fix-shape hint:). Repro at head: an original whose Confirmed findings body is `[Should-fix] a.sh:1 — first claim (new) [lanes: correctness]` / `  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓)` / `  evidence: seats reproduced` (no `- ` marker) fails --complete with exit 4. The correct repair, `- ` on the first line only, passes --complete with exactly one keyed occurrence, but --repair-check exits 4 with `(iv) line 5 unmarked line becomes a continuation:   verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓)`. Marking every line instead fails (iii). So no marker-only repair can pass. At 487992e1 the same pair was accepted (exit 0). The cause: --classify labels every line after the first unmarked line as `unmarked`, because no item is open, so the indented continuation lines of the stranded finding are indistinguishable from a second stranded finding. Impact: design §4.1(c) says \"a stranded confirmed finding can be recovered\". For the template shape it now never can be, and the round falls back to the loud `complete extraction unavailable` gap. This fails safe (original stands, nothing silently lost), but it disables the feature's main use case. The suite stays green because every stranded-finding fixture (test-report-verdict.sh:719-728, :801-802) is single-line. Fix shape: limit the new rejection to lines that would be keyed findings themselves (e.g. a roast `[SEV] location` lead in Confirmed findings) or to unindented lines, and add a test that repairs a multi-line template-shaped stranded finding."},{"section":"Must fix before landing","position":1,"key":"skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:298 (mlr.27): the new (iv) \"unmarked line becomes a 487992e1 accepted; narrow the rule to finding-shaped lines and add a multi-line stranded-finding test.","text":"skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:298 (mlr.27): the new (iv) \"unmarked line - finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"roast-code/round-4","artifact":"2026-10-28-review-continuity-followups-roast-pr-4.md","kind":"roast","verdict":"Should-fix (1 confirmed) [low coverage] [review readiness incomplete]","occurrences":[{"section":"Confirmed findings","position":1,"key":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:298","text":"[Should-fix] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:298 — The rule (iv) check mlr.27 (fc134cb2) added (`ra.role === 'unmarked' && b === a && rb.role === 'continuation'`) rejects every marker-only repair of a stranded confirmed finding that has indented continuation lines, which is the reporter template's normal verdict/evidence/fix-shape-hint shape; marking the continuation lines too fails (iii), so no repair can pass, the design §4.1(c) recovery promise is dead for template-shaped reports, the original stays the round's report path with a `complete extraction unavailable` gap, the design was not amended to record the new rule, and the added tests cover only single-line findings. (new) [lanes: correctness, premortem, simplicity-design, regression, api-contract, observability, testing, test-causality-lifecycle, hygiene-docs, deploy-safety] [fix-regression]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: All three seats reproduced at head 9b1582f8 with the script extracted via `git show`. Original: roast header `super-roast verdict: Should-fix (1 confirmed)`, `## Confirmed findings`, unmarked `[Should-fix] a.sh:1 — first claim`, then indented `  verdict: confirmed` and `  evidence: x`, then `## Rejected (with reason)` / `- none`. `--complete` on the original exits 4 (\"finding text without a list marker\"). `--classify` gives the finding line and its indented lines all role `unmarked`, because no item is open before them. Repair A adds `- ` to the finding line only: `--complete` exits 0 with one keyed occurrence `[Should-fix] a.sh:1` holding all the lines, `--keys` prints that key, but `--repair-check o.md r.md` exits 4 with `(iv) line 5 unmarked line becomes a continuation:   verdict: confirmed`. Repair B also prefixes the indented lines: exits 4 with `(iii) confirmed finding without a --keys key: verdict: confirmed`, the split that the existing test at tests/super-code/test-report-verdict.sh:634-635 already rejects. So no repair rule (iv) allows can pass this shape. Control: a single-line stranded finding still passes at head, and the same Repair A exits 0 at fc134cb2^. The shape is the normal one: skills/super-roast/reporter-prompt.md:247-250 gives every confirmed finding indented `verdict:`, `evidence:` and `fix-shape hint:` lines. Design §4.1(c) at head says \"a stranded confirmed finding can be recovered (an unmarked `[Should-fix] a.sh:3 — claim` prefixed with `- `...)\", states (iv) as text equality plus the (section, inFence) sequence, says an unmarked line \"may be prefixed\", and §4.1(a) accepts folding an unindented line into the preceding item as a residual; no \"becomes a continuation\" wording exists in design.md or amend-code-1.md, only in the script header (line 71) and line 298. Tests fc134cb2 added (test-report-verdict.sh ~L715-728, `rc_cf`) use only single-line findings. Caller effect (skills/super-roast/SKILL.md:236-239): the one reporter re-dispatch is wasted, the original stays the round's report path and the round records a loud `complete extraction unavailable` gap instead of keyed occurrences. Severity: all three seats Should-fix. The Blocking floor for \"violation of the artifact's own stated core purpose\" was considered and not applied: the failure is loud, nothing is dropped, and the run falls back to the pre-repair bookkeeping rather than mis-reporting; what is defeated is the format-repair sub-step, not the review bookkeeping itself. No profile demotion applied. Cause: `--classify` cannot tell a stranded finding's own continuation lines from a second stranded finding, and mlr.27 attached a behavioural rule to that role without a multi-line fixture. New relative to iteration 3; it is the regression of the :290 fix.\n  fix-shape hint: narrow the line-298 rejection to lines that are themselves finding-shaped (unindented, or matching `[SEV] path:line —`), or to the case where the repair's Confirmed-findings key count drops below the verdict's confirmed count; add a template-shaped multi-line stranded-finding fixture that expects exit 0 next to the two-finding fold fixture that expects exit 4; amend design §4.1(c) to record whichever rule is kept."}]}

- finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"roast-code/round-5","artifact":"2026-10-28-review-continuity-followups-roast-pr-5.md","kind":"roast","verdict":"clean (0 nits) [low coverage] [review readiness incomplete]","occurrences":[{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":1,"key":"[FYI] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:258","text":"[FYI] skills/super-code/scripts/report-verdict:258 — 053298f2 deleted the (iv) \"unmarked line becomes a continuation\" rejection, so `--repair-check` now accepts a repair that marks only the first of two stranded `## Escalations (need human)` entries and folds the second into it (same for Rejected and Unverified nits); claimed to contradict the amended design's \"Folds are caught by the count in (iii)\". [lanes: regression] [fix-regression]\n  panel: reproduce REJECT (FYI) / refute REJECT (FYI) / ground CONFIRM (Should-fix). Default route rejected (2 REJECT); no overrule, no escalation.\n  reason: The mechanism is undisputed. All three seats agree that at head a marker-only repair of two unmarked Escalations lines passes `--repair-check` and yields one occurrence holding both lines. The seats disagree on whether that violates a stated requirement, and the majority's evidence answers the minority's citations.\n  - The reproduce seat quotes design §4.1(c): check (iii) is defined as \"the repair recovers exactly the original's confirmed findings\", and the sentence \"Folds are caught by the count in (iii)\" sits in the passage about stranded confirmed findings (the `(2 confirmed)` example). amend-code-2.md:137 likewise names only the `(2 confirmed)` two-finding fold. No spec text promises fold protection for Rejected, Unverified nits or Escalations.\n  - Both rejecting seats cite design §4.1(a)'s accepted residual: an unindented finding line after an item is folded into the preceding item's text, \"kept verbatim in that occurrence, not dropped, but given no key of its own\". That residual applies to every recognized section and already exists under plain `--complete` with no repair involved. The repaired Escalations shape is exactly that accepted residual; no text is lost.\n  - The refute seat notes §4.1(c) gives the rationale for dropping (iv) outright: the per-line rule cannot tell a folded sibling from a template continuation line, and that rationale holds in every section. Narrowing the rule to non-Confirmed sections is not specified.\n  - The ground seat's dissent cites super-auto/SKILL.md:399 and :454 (each Escalations entry gets its own `finding-route` and `parked:` line by occurrence ref, so a folded second escalation gets no human gate) and argues §4.1(a) governs the extractor, not the repair gate. The majority addresses this directly: the routing consequence is a downstream effect of the §4.1(a) residual the design already accepts, not a new requirement violation. The dissent's remaining citations (design §4.1(c), amend-code-2.md:137) are the same text the majority read in context, so the seat evidence does not leave the point unsettled.\n  - Preconditions are narrow: the reporter must strand two Escalations entries unmarked and the repair agent must mark only one; the producer rule that each finding is its own `- ` item is the guard.\n  - What survives, per the refute seat, is a documentation and test-coverage observation, not a confirmed defect: the design's \"Folds are caught by the count in (iii)\" sentence and amend-code-2.md:137's \"repairs they reject stay rejected, except the two-finding fold\" are literally true only for Confirmed findings, and no fixture exercises a non-Confirmed fold. If the maintainer wants fold protection outside Confirmed findings, that is a new requirement to write into the design, not a regression against it.\n  - Not pre-existing on base: main@444f86b2 has no `--repair-check` at all."}]}
